> *he ṛtvigyajamānā vo yuṣmadarthaṃ sanā sanātanāni tā tāni kā cit kāni*
> *cit brahmaṇaspateḥ prajñānāni bhuvanā bhuvanānāṃ ‖ supāṃ sulugiti ṣaṣṭhyā luk ‖*
> *bhuvanānāmudakānāṃ bhavītvā bhāvyānāṃ duro dvārāṇi mādbhirmāsaiḥ ‖ paddannityā-*
> *dinā māsaśabdasya māsbhāvaḥ | svatavasthāsoṣasāṃ ca ta iṣyate | pā. 7-4-48-2. |*
> *iti tatvaṃ ‖ śaradbhiḥ saṃvatsaraiśca varaṃta | nivṛṇvaṃti | tatkāle varṣaṇārthaṃ meghadvārā-*
> *ṇyudvāṭayaṃtītyarthaḥ| kāni punastāni| brahmaṇaspatiyāryāni vayunā‖ jñānanāmaitat ‖*
> *jñānāni maṃtraviṣayāṇi cakāra | kṛtavān | tadanaṃtaramayataṃtā ayatamānāva-*
> *prayatnāvubhau lok dauśca pṛthivī ca anyadanyadit anyadanyadeva pṛthivī vṛṣṭijalaṃ*
> *divyamudakaṃ dyaurudakakāryaṃ hanirlakṣaṇaṃ pārthivamannaṃ evaṃ parasparavyatihāreṇa*
> *carataḥ | bhuṃjāte ‖ cara gatibhakṣaṇayoḥ ‖*

Gist: the eternal wisdom-deeds of Brahmaṇaspati open, month by month and year by year, the doors of the coming waters (rain) for the sacrificers; heaven and earth, unexerted, exchange water and offering-food.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(O priests and sacrificers) ब्रह्मणस्पतिः (*brahmaṇaspatiḥ*)—Brahmaṇaspati; या (*yā*)—which; वयुना (*vayunā*)—knowledge-endowed acts; चकार (*cakāra*)—did (as the fruit of the mantra recitation); सना (*sanā*)—ancient; ता का चित् (*tā kā cit*)—those acts, of many forms and renowned; भुवना (*bhuvanā*)—of the waters; भवित्वा (*bhavitvā*) (as supplied; the Pada and Saṃhitā have भवीत्वा (*bhavītvā*))—of the future; दुरः (*duraḥ*)—the doors; मार्धिः (*mārdhiḥ*) (as supplied; the Pada has मार्त्भिः (*mārtbhiḥ*))—during the span of months; शरद्भिः (*śaradbhiḥ*)—during the span of years; वः (*vaḥ*)—for your use; वरंत (*varaṃta*)—they open (they make a way for the flow of the waters). (Thereafter) अयतंता (*ayataṃtā*)—heaven and earth, which act of their own impulse without effort; अन्यदन्यदित् (*anyadanyadit*)—the foods given to each other (the water given by the heaven-world to the earth, and the food in the form of oblation given by the earth to the heaven-world); चरतः (*carataḥ*)—they make use of.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O priests and sacrificers, those acts, endowed with knowledge, that Brahmaṇaspati accomplished as the fruit of reciting the mantra, ancient, of many kinds and renowned, open the doors of the future of the waters, during the span of months and of years, for your use [the Bhāvārtha continues on p. 24, in the next window].

### Printed p. 24

*Running head: [24 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, Ā. 3, Sū. 24] (as supplied).*

(Bhāvārtha of Ṛk 5, continued from p. 23) ... and make a way for the flow of the waters. Thereafter heaven and earth, which act of their own impulse without any restraint, mutually enjoy the foods given to each other; that is, the earth enjoys the water given by the heaven-world, and the heaven-world enjoys the food, in the form of oblation, given by the earth.

**Printed English**

For you, (worshippers), the constant and manifold (bounties of Brahmanaspati), through months and through years, set open the gates of the future rains, and he has appointed those results of prayer, which the two regions mutually and without effort provide.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The meaning of this Ṛk is somewhat difficult. Many have explained it in many ways. For the word भुवना (*bhuvanā*) in this Ṛk some take it to mean the people of the world, while Sāyaṇa takes it to mean water. Likewise he has supplied the word "heaven and earth" (द्यावापृथिविगळु (*dyāvāpṛthivigaḷu*)) by ellipsis and explained it so that the sense fits.

ब्रह्मणः पतिः या वयुना चकार सना ता का चित् भुवना भवित्वा दुरः माद्भिः शरद्धिः वः वरंत (*brahmaṇaḥ patiḥ yā vayunā cakāra sanā tā kā cit bhuvanā bhavitvā duraḥ mādbhiḥ śaraddhiḥ vaḥ varaṃta*)—When Brahmaṇaspati made knowledge the subject of the mantra, that is, made it public in the form of the meaning of the mantra, the knowledge-endowed mantras of Brahmaṇaspati (the sacrificial acts and the like), in the months and in the years, for your (the people's) benefit, caused the doors of the rain-water to be opened and the rain to fall. By reciting meaningful mantras in sacrifices and the like, rain falls, as the fruit of it, throughout the year, in all the months, that is, during the greater part of the year. The main idea is that the water stored in the clouds is released by the recitation of meaningful mantras in sacrifices and the like, and falls on the earth in the form of rain. Here the word वयुन (*vayuna*) is read among the names of wisdom (Nir. 3-13), the word भुवन (*bhuvana*) among the names of water (Nir. 2-24); the word शरत् (*śarat*) is used in the sense of year (Nir. 4-26).

अयतंता अन्यदन्यत् इत् चरतः (*ayataṃtā anyadanyat it carataḥ*)—By the greatness of Brahmaṇaspati, heaven and earth, without any effort of their own, cooperate with each other and work. How? The doors of the water that is to rain in future (the clouds) are broken open in the respective months or years and made to pour rain; that is, this Brahmaṇaspati, by his power of mantra, makes the clouds pour rain at the proper time, and makes heaven and earth dependent on one another. In due season rain [the sentence continues on p. 25]

### Printed p. 25

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 2] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 25 (as supplied).*

(continued from p. 24) ... falls, plants and the like grow well on the earth, and with their help the people are enabled to perform sacrifices and the like. The gods in the heaven-world accept the oblations and the like given by men, are satisfied, and bring about rain. This in turn favours the sacrifices and the like. Therefore the heaven-world's pouring of rain, and the earth's producing plants and the like and helping the sacrificial acts, is called the mutual dependence of heaven and earth. Since this work goes on without effort on the part of heaven and earth, the main idea is that the power of mantra, or the greatness, of Brahmaṇaspati is the cause of such works.

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अ॒भि॒नक्षं॑तो अ॒भि ये तमा॑न॒शुर्नि॒धिं प॑णी॒नां प॑र॒मं गुहा॑ हि॒तं ।
> ते वि॒द्वांसः॑ प्रति॒चक्ष्यानृ॑ता॒ पुन॒र्यत॑ उ॒ आयं॒तदुदी॑युरा॒विशं॑ ॥

> *abhinakṣaṃto abhi ye tamānaśurnidhiṃ paṇīnāṃ paramaṃ guhā hitaṃ |*
> *te vidvāṃsaḥ praticakṣyānṛtā punaryata u āyaṃtadudīyurāviśaṃ ‖*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अभिनक्षंतः । अभि । ये । तं । आनशुः । निधिं । पणीनां । परमं । गुहा । हितं ।
> ते । विद्वांसः । प्रतिचक्ष्य । अनृता । पुनः । यतः । ऊं इति । आयन् । तत् । उत् ।
> ईयुः । अविशं ॥ ६ ॥

> *abhinakṣaṃtaḥ | abhi | ye | taṃ | ānaśuḥ | nidhiṃ | paṇīnāṃ | paramaṃ | guhā | hitaṃ |*
> *te | vidvāṃsaḥ | praticakṣya | anṛtā | punaḥ | yataḥ | ūṃ iti | āyan | tat | ut |*
> *īyuḥ | aviśaṃ ‖ 6 ‖*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> गोसमूहे पणिभिरसुरैरपहृते सति पणीनां स्थानं सरमाख्यया देवशुन्या
> ज्ञात्वा ब्रह्मणस्पतिना सृष्ट्वा अंगिरसस्तत्स्थानं प्राप्य गोसमूहं पणिभिर्निर्मिता
> मायाश्च दृष्ट्वा असुरनिवासस्थानं स्वहस्तोद्भूतेनाग्निना दग्धा प्रत्यागच्छन् ।
> तदेद्द्वचेनोच्यते । अभिनक्षंतोऽभितो गच्छंतो येऽंगिरसस्तं निधिं गोस-
> मूहरूपं निधानं धनं परममुत्कृष्टं पणीनां ॥ त्रितीयार्थे षष्ठी ।
> पणिभिर्गुहायां निहितं स्थापितमभ्यानतुः । अभिलक्ष्यानशिरे । प्राप्तवंतः ॥

> *gosamūhe paṇibhirasurairapahṛte sati paṇīnāṃ sthānaṃ saramākhyayā devaśunyā*
> *jñātvā brahmaṇaspatinā sṛṣṭvā aṃgirasastatsthānaṃ prāpya gosamūhaṃ paṇibhirnirmitā*
> *māyāśca dṛṣṭvā asuranivāsasthānaṃ svahastodbhūtenāgninā dagdhā pratyāgacchan |*
> *tadeddvacenocyate | abhinakṣaṃto 'bhito gacchaṃto ye 'ṃgirasastaṃ nidhiṃ gosa-*
> *mūharūpaṃ nidhānaṃ dhanaṃ paramamutkṛṣṭaṃ paṇīnāṃ ‖ tritīyārthe ṣaṣṭhī |*
> *paṇibhirguhāyāṃ nihitaṃ sthāpitamabhyānatuḥ | abhilakṣyānaśire | prāptavaṃtaḥ ‖*

### Printed p. 26

*Running head: [26 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24] (as supplied).*

> विद्वांसोऽभिज्ञास्तेऽंगिरसोऽनृता अनृतान्यसुरैः कृता मायाः प्रतिचक्ष्य दृष्ट्वा-
> आविशमावेष्टुं प्रवेष्टुं यत उ यत एव स्थानादायन् आगच्छन् तत्स्थानं
> पुनरुदीयुः । उदगच्छन् । उत्कर्षेण प्राप्ताः ॥

> *vidvāṃso 'bhijñāste 'ṃgiraso 'nṛtā anṛtānyasuraiḥ kṛtā māyāḥ praticakṣya dṛṣṭvā-*
> *āviśamāveṣṭuṃ praveṣṭuṃ yata u yata eva sthānādāyan āgacchan tatsthānaṃ*
> *punarudīyuḥ | udagacchan | utkarṣeṇa prāptāḥ ‖*

Gist: when the Paṇi asuras had stolen the cows, the Aṅgirases, guided by Sarama the divine bitch sent by Brahmaṇaspati, found the Paṇis' place, saw their illusions, burned them with fire from their hands, and returned.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अभिनक्षंतः (*abhinakṣaṃtaḥ*)—searching on every side (for the cows); ये (*ye*)—which Aṅgirases; परमं (*paramaṃ*)—excellent; तं निधिं (*taṃ nidhiṃ*)—that treasure, in the form of cows; पणीनां (*paṇīnāṃ*)—of the Paṇis; गुहा हितं (*guhā hitaṃ*)—placed in the cave; अभि आनशुः (*abhi ānaśuḥ*)—discovered; विद्वांसः (*vidvāṃsaḥ*)—the wise; ते (*te*)—those Aṅgirases; अनृता (*anṛtā*)—that illusion of the Asuras; पतिचक्ष्य (*paticakṣya*) (as supplied; the Saṃhitā and Pada have प्रतिचक्ष्य (*praticakṣya*))—having seen (the cave in the form of illusion); अविशं (*aviśaṃ*)—to enter; यत उ (*yata u*)—from whichever place; आयन् (*āyan*)—they had come; तत् (*tat*)—that very place; पुनः (*punaḥ*)—again; उदीयुः (*udīyuḥ*)—went and reached exceedingly.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Those Aṅgirases who, searching on every side for the cows, discovered that excellent treasure, in the form of cows, placed in the cave of the Paṇis—those same wise Aṅgirases, having seen that illusion of the Asuras, the cave in the form of illusion, went again exceedingly and reached the very place from which they had come in order to enter it. (The supplied text has उष्कृष्टवादुदू (*uṣkṛṣṭavādudū*) for "excellent"; the Pratipadārtha has उत्कृष्टवादुदू (*utkṛṣṭavādudū*); kept as supplied.)

**Printed English**

Those sages, who, searching on every side, discoverd [sic] the precious treasure (of cattle) hidden in the cave of the Panis, having seen through the false (illusions of the Asura), and again coming (thither), forced an entrance.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk and in the next Ṛk the subject of Brahmaṇaspati or Bṛhaspati is not taken up. But the praise of the Aṅgirā sages, the descendants of Bṛhaspati, and a description of the daring deeds they did on the occasion of bringing the cows stolen by the Paṇis, are stated. To explain the meaning of this Ṛk the bhāṣyakāra has briefly set out here the story told in other places. Formerly the asuras called Paṇis stole the herd of cows of the gods (or of the Aṅgirā sages?) and kept it hidden in a secret place, that is, in a mountain cave. To search for these cows Indra sent the divine bitch called Saramā (the divine bitch of the world of the gods ... [the passage continues in the next window].

### Printed p. 27

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 2] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 27 (as supplied).*

(continued from p. 26) ... bitch) to find them. That bitch travelled a very long way, crossed the deep and brimming river called Rasā, came to the place where the Paṇis were, conversed with them, discovered the place where the cows had been hidden, and informed the gods. This matter is described at length in Ṛg-veda Saṃhitā, Maṇḍala 10, Sūkta 108. Afterwards the Aṅgirā sages, created by Brahmaṇaspati, went to the mountain cave where the Paṇis had hidden the cows, removed the many kinds of magical snares the Asuras had made, and came near the place where the cows were. They also set fire to the dwellings of the Asuras, burned them to ashes, and brought the cows back. The bhāṣyakāra has set out this matter as an introduction (pīṭhikā) to what is said in this Ṛk.

अभिनक्षंतः ये पणीनां गुहा हितं परमं तं निधिं अभि आनशुः (*abhinakṣaṃtaḥ ye paṇīnāṃ guhā hitaṃ paramaṃ taṃ nidhiṃ abhi ānaśuḥ*)—अभितो गच्छंतो येंगिरसः पणिभिर्गुहायां स्थापितमुत्कृष्टं गोसमूहरूपं धनं अभिलक्ष्य प्राप्तवंतः (*abhito gacchaṃto yeṃgirasaḥ paṇibhirguhāyāṃ sthāpitamutkṛṣṭaṃ gosamūharūpaṃ dhanaṃ abhilakṣya prāptavaṃtaḥ*) — The Aṅgirā sages, while going about searching for the cows stolen by the Paṇis, learned that that treasure in the form of cows lay hidden by the Paṇis in a cave, a secret place, and came there. In this sentence the words अभिनक्षंतः ये (*abhinakṣaṃtaḥ ye*) mean only "those who go searching", but since in many contexts it is said that those who search for the cows in this way are the Aṅgirā sages, it must be inferred that here too the Aṅgirā sages are meant. Although the word पणीनां (*paṇīnāṃ*) is in the genitive case, the bhāṣyakāra explains that it must be taken in the sense of the instrumental case, that is, in the sense "by the Paṇis, the Asuras". The word निधिं (*nidhiṃ*) means the wealth in the form of a herd of cows. For, since it is said plainly पणीनां (पणिभिः) गुहा हितं (*paṇīnāṃ (paṇibhiḥ) guhā hitaṃ*) ("the treasure hidden by the Paṇis in the cave"), and since the Paṇis had hidden only cows, the word निधि (*nidhi*) here means wealth in the form of cows, that is, the cows themselves.

ते विद्वांसः अनृता प्रतिचक्ष्य आविशं यतः ऊं आयन् पुनः तत् उत् ईयुः (*te vidvāṃsaḥ anṛtā praticakṣya āviśaṃ yataḥ ūṃ āyan punaḥ tat ut īyuḥ*)—अभिज्ञास्त्येऽर्जसोऽनृतान्यसुरैः कृता मायाः दृष्ट्वा प्रवेष्टुं यत एव स्थानादागच्छन् तत् स्थानं पुनः उत्कर्षण प्राप्ताः (*abhijñāstye 'rjaso 'nṛtānyasuraiḥ kṛtā māyāḥ dṛṣṭvā praveṣṭuṃ yata eva sthānādāgacchan tat sthānaṃ punaḥ utkarṣaṇa prāptāḥ*) (as supplied; the bhāṣya printed above has अभिज्ञास्तेऽंगिरसोऽनृता अनृतान्यसुरैः (*abhijñāste 'ṃgiraso 'nṛtā anṛtānyasuraiḥ*) and उत्कर्षेण (*utkarṣeṇa*) here). Because they knew everything, those Aṅgirā sages came again to the place which they had already come and learned beforehand — how to enter the secret place where the Paṇis, by tricks and devices, had hidden the cows so that no one knew. The sense is: the Aṅgirā sages knew by their discernment the cave in which the Paṇis had hidden the cows; even so, they had already looked at that cave and examined it beforehand and learned where and how it should be entered. Accordingly they came again to a place convenient for entering that cave first.

### Printed p. 28

*Running head: [28 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24] (as supplied).*

Besides this meaning, another, different meaning can be given to this Ṛk. It also appears to suggest that the Aṅgirā sages, who know everything, by the power of their sacrificial rites made rain fall, when the clouds (अभिनक्षंतः (*abhinakṣaṃtaḥ*)) moving in the atmosphere were holding back, without pouring it down on the earth as rain, the water stored in them as treasure.

#### Ṛk 7

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ऋ॒तावा॑नः प्रति॒चक्ष्यान्वि॑ता॒ पुन॒रात॒ आ त॑स्थुः क॒वयो॑ म॒हस्प॒थः ।
> ते बा॒हुभ्यां॑ धमि॒तम॒ग्निमश्म॑नि॒ नकिः॒ षो अ॒स्त्यर॑णो ज॒हुर्हि तं॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ऋतवानः । प्रतिचक्ष्य । अनृता । पुनः । आ । अतः । आ ; तस्तुः । कवयः । महः ।
> पथः ।
> ते । बाहुभ्यां । धमितं । अग्निं । अश्मनि । नकिः । सः । अस्ति । अरणः । जहुः ।
> हि । तं ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> ऋतावानः । ऋतमिति सत्यनामधेयं । तद्वंतः कवयः क्रांतदर्शिनः सर्वज्ञा
> अंगीरसोऽनृता अनृतानि प्रतिचक्ष्य पूर्वोक्तप्रकारेण दृष्ट्वा अतोऽस्मात्स्थानात्
> पुनराजिगमषवो महस्पथो महतो मार्गान् प्रति आतस्थुः । अवतस्थिरे ।
> अवतिष्ठमानाश्च ते बाहुभ्यां हस्ताभ्यां धमितं मंथनोत्पादितं प्रज्वालितमग्निम-
> श्मनि पणीनां निवासभूते शिलोच्चये जहुर्हि। दग्दुं त्यक्तवंतः खलु । अरणोऽर
> मयिता दाहेन दुःखकारी सोऽग्निः पूर्वं तत्र नकिरस्ति । न विद्यते । अविद्यमान-
> मग्निं स्वसामर्थ्यादुत्पाद्य तत्र प्रचिक्षिपुरित्यर्थः ॥

> *ṛtāvānaḥ | ṛtamiti satyanāmadheyaṃ | tadvaṃtaḥ kavayaḥ krāṃtadarśinaḥ sarvajñā*
> *aṃgīraso 'nṛtā anṛtāni praticakṣya pūrvoktaprakāreṇa dṛṣṭvā ato 'smātsthānāt*
> *punarājigamaṣavo mahaspatho mahato mārgān prati ātasthuḥ | avatasthire |*
> *avatiṣṭhamānāśca te bāhubhyāṃ hastābhyāṃ dhamitaṃ maṃthanotpāditaṃ prajvālitamagnima-*
> *śmani paṇīnāṃ nivāsabhūte śiloccaye jahurhi| dagduṃ tyaktavaṃtaḥ khalu | araṇo 'ra*
> *mayitā dāhena duḥkhakārī so 'gniḥ pūrvaṃ tatra nakirasti | na vidyate | avidyamāna-*
> *magniṃ svasāmarthyādutpādya tatra pracikṣipurityarthaḥ ‖*

Gist: the truthful, all-seeing Aṅgirases, having seen the Asuras' illusions, turned back to the great paths and cast fire, kindled by churning with their arms, on the Paṇis' rocky dwelling, where no such fire had been before.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ऋतावानः (*ṛtāvānaḥ*)—endowed with truth; कवयः (*kavayaḥ*)—far-seeing (all-knowing) Aṅgirases; अनृता (*anṛtā*)—the false (illusions of the Asuras); प्रतिचक्ष्य (*praticakṣya*)—having seen; अतः (*ataḥ*)—from that place; पुनः (*punaḥ*)—wishing to return; महस्पथः (*mahaspathaḥ*)—to the great road; आ तस्थुः (*ā tasthuḥ*)—

### Printed p. 29

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 2] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 29 (as supplied).*

(continued from p. 28) ... came and stood; ते (*te*)—they; बाहुभ्यां (*bāhubhyāṃ*)—with their arms; धमितं (*dhamitaṃ*)—churned and kindled; तं अग्निं (*taṃ agniṃ*)—that fire; आत्मनी (*ātmanī*) (as supplied; the Saṃhitā and Pada have अश्मनि (*aśmani*))—on the rocks of the mountain (the dwelling of the Paṇis); जहुर्हि (*jahurhi*)—they placed; अरणः (*araṇaḥ*)—[the gloss is printed वहिसतक्क (*vahisatakka*), "bearing"; the Bhāvārtha has "burning"] [?]; सः (*saḥ*)—that fire; नकिः अस्ति (*nakiḥ asti*)—(before) was not there at all.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The Aṅgirases, endowed with truth and all-knowing, having seen the illusions of the Asuras, which were untrue, and wishing to return from that place, came and stood on the great road. They placed the fire, churned with their arms and blazing, on the rocks of the mountain, the dwelling of the Paṇis. The burning fire had not been there before.

**Printed English**

Those sages, eminent for truth, having seen through the false (illusions of the Asura), again pursued the main road thither, and with their hands cast against the rock the destructive fire, which, till then, was not there.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ऋतावानः कवयः अनृता प्रतिचक्ष्य अतः पुनः महस्पथः आ तस्थुः (*ṛtāvānaḥ kavayaḥ anṛtā praticakṣya ataḥ punaḥ mahaspathaḥ ā tasthuḥ*)—Those Āṅgirā sages, truthful and far-seeing, that is, all-knowing, having seen the magical snares of the Asuras, found no way to enter inside the cave where the cows were hidden, and came and stood on the great road by which they had come. But they did not wish to go away without doing the task for which they had come. Therefore,

ते बाहुभ्यां धमितं तं अग्निं अश्मनि जहुर्हि (*te bāhubhyāṃ dhamitaṃ taṃ agniṃ aśmani jahurhi*)—आतिष्ठमानाश्च ते हस्ताभ्यां मंथनोत्पादितं प्रज्वालितमग्निं पणीनां निवासभूतेशिलोच्चये दग्धुं त्यक्तवंतः खलु (*ātiṣṭhamānāśca te hastābhyāṃ maṃthanotpāditaṃ prajvālitamagniṃ paṇīnāṃ nivāsabhūteśiloccaye dagdhuṃ tyaktavaṃtaḥ khalu*). They strongly churned with their hands and produced fire, and while it blazed they set it alight in the dwellings on the mountain where the Paṇis lived, and made it burn. In general, when enemies are to be defeated in war and the like, it is even now the custom to set fire to their dwellings and cause them immense loss. Thereby the enemies are terrified and defeated. Following this method, the Aṅgirā sages churned out fire, and as soon as it was well kindled and began to blaze, they set fire to the town of the enemy Paṇis, or to their huts: this is the sense.

### Printed p. 30

*Running head: [30 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24] (as supplied; the word "bhāṣya" is misprinted सायणभाव्यसहिता (*sāyaṇabhāvyasahitā*) in the supplied text). The supplied text has no separate page head for p. 30; its contents were found inside p. 29 and are taken as p. 30.*

अरणः सः नकिः अस्ति (*araṇaḥ saḥ nakiḥ asti*)—अरणो अरमयिता । दाहेन दुःखकारी सः अग्निः पूर्वं तत्र न विद्यते (*araṇo aramayitā | dāhena duḥkhakārī saḥ agniḥ pūrvaṃ tatra na vidyate*). In many places रण (*raṇa*) is explained as "delightful". अरणः (*araṇaḥ*) therefore means "not delightful". Here, because the heat of fire causes suffering in the form of burning and the like, fire is described as अरणः (*araṇaḥ*). Since fire has the nature of burning, it causes people suffering; that is, when dwellings are burning with fire, it is natural for people to feel great sorrow. Such a distress-causing [?] fire was not there before.

(The supplied text has here an ornamental picture, described by the text-reading software as containing no text; it is not reproduced.)

#### Ṛk 8

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ऋ॒तज्ये॑न क्षि॒प्रेण॒ ब्रह्म॑ण॒स्पति॒र्यत्र॒ वष्टि॒ प्र तद॑श्नोति॒ धन्व॑ना ।
> तस्य॑ सा॒ध्वरिष॑वो॒ याभि॒रस्य॑ति नृ॒चक्ष॑सो दृ॒शये॒ कर्ण॑योनयः ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ऋतज्येन । क्षिप्रेण । ब्रह्मणः । पतिः । यत्र । वष्टि । प्र । तत् । अश्नोति । धन्वना ।
> तस्य । साध्वीः । इषवः । याभिः । अस्यति । नृचक्षसः । दृशये । कर्णयोनयः ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> ऋतं सत्यमेव ज्या यस्य तेन क्षिप्रेणेषूणां क्षेपकेण धन्वना धरणभूतेन
> धनुषा ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्ब्रह्मणो मंत्रस्य पालयिता देवो यत्र यस्मिन्विषये वष्टि
> कामयते, तत्पाश्नोति । प्रकर्षेण व्याप्प्नोति ॥ अश्नोतेर्व्यत्ययेन परस्मैपदं ॥
> तस्य धनुषः साध्वीः साधिकास्ता इषवो भवंति याभिरिषुभिर्ब्रह्मणस्पतिरस्यति । राक्षसा-
> दीन् क्षिपति । कीदृश्यस्ताः । नृचक्षसो नृन् पश्यतः । उपलक्षणमेतत् । सर्वज्ञस्य
> ब्रह्मणस्पतेर्द्र्यशये दर्शनाय ज्ञानाय जाताः कर्णयोनयः श्रोत्रेंद्रियेण
> ग्राह्या मंत्रभूता आकर्णकृष्णा वा बाणाः । ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्मंत्रैरेव सर्वं साधय-
> तीति भावः ॥

> *ṛtaṃ satyameva jyā yasya tena kṣipreṇeṣūṇāṃ kṣepakeṇa dhanvanā dharaṇabhūtena*
> *dhanuṣā brahmaṇaspatirbrahmaṇo maṃtrasya pālayitā devo yatra yasminviṣaye vaṣṭi*
> *kāmayate, tatpāśnoti | prakarṣeṇa vyāppnoti ‖ aśnotervyatyayena parasmaipadaṃ ‖*
> *tasya dhanuṣaḥ sādhvīḥ sādhikāstā iṣavo bhavaṃti yābhiriṣubhirbrahmaṇaspatirasyati | rākṣasā-*
> *dīn kṣipati | kīdṛśyastāḥ | nṛcakṣaso nṛn paśyataḥ | upalakṣaṇametat | sarvajñasya*
> *brahmaṇaspaterdryaśaye darśanāya jñānāya jātāḥ karṇayonayaḥ śrotreṃdriyeṇa*
> *grāhyā maṃtrabhūtā ākarṇakṛṣṇā vā bāṇāḥ | brahmaṇaspatirmaṃtraireva sarvaṃ sādhaya-*
> *tīti bhāvaḥ ‖*

Gist: Brahmaṇaspati, with a quick-shooting bow whose string is truth, attains whatever he desires; his arrows, perceived through the ear (the mantras), accomplish everything.

### Printed p. 31

*Running head: [A. 2 A. 7 V. 2.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 31 (as supplied). The window begins inside Ṛk 8, at the Pratipadārtha.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ऋतज्येन (ऋतज्येन (*ṛtajyena*))—with the one whose bowstring is truth; क्षिप्रेण (क्षिप्रेण (*kṣipreṇa*))—that shoots arrows swiftly; धन्वना (धन्वना (*dhanvanā*))—with the bow; ब्रह्मणस्पतिः (ब्रह्मणस्पतिः (*brahmaṇaspatiḥ*))—Brahmaṇaspati; यत्र (यत्र (*yatra*))—at whatever target; वष्टि (वष्टि (*vaṣṭi*))—takes aim and desires; तत् (तत् (*tat*))—that; प्र अश्नोति (प्र अश्नोति (*pra aśnoti*))—he attains fully; याभिः (याभिः (*yābhiḥ*))—with which arrows; अस्यति (अस्यति (*asyati*))—Bṛhaspati [sic: so in the Kannada] overthrows the Rākṣasas and the like, such (arrows); नृचक्षसः (नृचक्षसः (*nṛcakṣasaḥ*))—of the all-knowing Brahmaṇaspati; दृशये (दृशये (*dṛśaye*))—produced for the sake of knowledge; कर्णयोनयः (कर्णयोनयः (*karṇayonayaḥ*))—perceptible by the organ of hearing, and of the nature of mantras; तस्य (तस्य (*tasya*))—of that bow; इषवः (इषवः (*iṣavaḥ*))—the arrows; साध्वीः (साध्वीः (*sādhvīḥ*))—accomplish their work.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

With the bow whose string is truth and which shoots arrows swiftly, Brahmaṇaspati fully attains whatever target he takes aim at and desires. The arrows of that bow, with which Bṛhaspati [so in the Kannada] overthrows the Rākṣasas and the like, which are produced for the knowledge of the all-knowing Brahmaṇaspati, and which are perceived by the organ of hearing and are of the nature of mantras, accomplish their work.

**Printed English**

Whatever Brahmanaspathi aims at with the truthrst-rung [sic] quick-darting bow, that (mark) he surely attains: holy are its arrows with which he shoots, (intended) for the eyes of men, and having their abode in the ear.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ब्रह्मणस्पतिः ऋतज्येन क्षिप्रेण धन्वना यत्र वष्टि तत् प्र अश्नोति (*brahmaṇaspatiḥ ṛtajyena kṣipreṇa dhanvanā yatra vaṣṭi tat pra aśnoti*)—सत्यमेव ज्या यस्य तेन इषूणां क्षेपकेण धनुषा ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्ब्रह्मणो मंत्रस्य पालयिता देवः यस्मिन् विषये कामयते तत्पकर्षेण प्राप्नोति (*satyameva jyā yasya tena iṣūṇāṃ kṣepakeṇa dhanuṣā brahmaṇaspatirbrahmaṇo maṃtrasya pālayitā devaḥ yasmin viṣaye kāmayate tatpakarṣeṇa prāpnoti*). Brahmaṇaspati is the god who protects the mantras; that is, with mantras he can accomplish any undertaking. The bowstring (ज्या (*jyā*)) of his bow is of the nature of truth; that is, truth itself is the string (cord) of his bow. Fitting such a string called truth to his bow, with arrows shot in quick succession (that is, with mantras), whatever matter Brahmaṇaspati aims at he accomplishes without fail. He does not miss the mark he has set; that is, he does not leave unaccomplished an undertaking he has resolved to carry out. Here the arrows are mantras, or the oblation offered to the gods. As the English translator Wilson says, "The arrows are oblations and mantras": the arrows are the oblation offered by him and the mantras uttered.

### Printed p. 32

*Running head: [32 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24] (as supplied; "bhāṣya" misspelt in the supplied text).*

There is nothing that cannot be accomplished by these. Therefore they carry the qualifying expression साद्वी8 (*sādvī8*) [?] (so in the supplied text; evidently साध्वीः (*sādhvīḥ*)), साधिकाः (*sādhikāḥ*), that is, "accomplishing the intended work".

नृचक्षसः कर्णयोनयः—नृन्‌पश्यतः, सर्वज्ञस्य बृहस्पतेः, श्रोत्रेंद्रियेण ग्राह्याः मंत्रभूता आकर्णकृष्टा वा बाणाः । ब्रह्मणस्पतिः मंत्रैरेव सर्वं साधयतीति भावः (*nṛcakṣasaḥ karṇayonayaḥ—nṛnpaśyataḥ, sarvajñasya bṛhaspateḥ, śrotreṃdriyeṇa grāhyāḥ maṃtrabhūtā ākarṇakṛṣṭā vā bāṇāḥ | brahmaṇaspatiḥ maṃtraireva sarvaṃ sādhayatīti bhāvaḥ*). Sāyaṇa understands नृचक्षसः (*nṛcakṣasaḥ*) as "seeing men", that is, "all-knowing", and कर्णयोनयः (*karṇayonayaḥ*) as "perceptible by the organ of hearing, of the nature of mantras, or arrows drawn back to the ear". Here he has applied नृचक्षसः (*nṛcakṣasaḥ*) to Brahmaṇaspati and stated his omniscience. But if we take the view of the English scholars cited above, the arrows are of two forms, oblation and mantra. Of these the oblations are नृचक्षसः (*nṛcakṣasaḥ*), visible to men, and the mantras are कर्णयोनयः (*karṇayonayaḥ*), to be heard by the ears of men [the Kannada reads किविगैकेळसतक्कवु (*kivigaikeḷasatakkavu*), apparently a misreading of "to be heard by the ear"]. Thus the mantras employed by Brahmaṇaspati and the oblations given are capable of doing the intended work. This Sāyaṇācārya has explained by the sentence ब्रह्मणस्पतिः मंत्रेणैव सर्वं साधयति (*brahmaṇaspatiḥ maṃtreṇaiva sarvaṃ sādhayati*) ("Brahmaṇaspati accomplishes everything by mantra alone"). The text ends with a numeral-like mark (as supplied: "-௨௰௪-", i.e. apparently the Sūkta number 24; not read further).

#### Ṛk 9

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> स सं॑न॒यः स वि॑न॒यः पु॒रोहि॑तः॒ स सुष्टु॑तः॒ स यु॒धि ब्रह्म॑ण॒स्पतिः॑ ।
> चा॒क्ष्मो यद्वाजं॒ भर॑ते म॒ती धनादित्सूर्य॑स्तपति तप्य॒तुर्वृथा॑ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सः । संनयः । सः । विनयः । पुरहितः । सः । सुस्तुतः । सः । युधि । ब्रह्मणः ।
> पतिः ।
> चाक्ष्मः । यत् । वाजं । भरते । मती । धना । आत् । इत् । सूर्यः । तपति ।
> तप्यतुः । वृथा ॥ ९ ॥

> *saḥ | saṃnayaḥ | saḥ | vinayaḥ | purahitaḥ | saḥ | sustutaḥ | saḥ | yudhi | brahmaṇaḥ |*
> *patiḥ |*
> *cākṣmaḥ | yat | vājaṃ | bharate | matī | dhanā | āt | it | sūryaḥ | tapati |*
> *tapyatuḥ | vṛthā ‖ 9 ‖*

### Printed p. 33

*Running head: [A. 2. A. 7. V. 2.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 33 (as supplied).*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> पुरोहितो देवैः पुरतोऽत्र स्थापितः स ब्रह्मणस्पतिः संनयः विश्लिष्टानां
> मंत्रैः संगमयिता भवति । विनयः संगतानां विविधं नेता पृथक्कर्तापि स एव ।
> तथा सुष्टुतः शोभनं स्तोतृभिः स्तुतश्च भवति । तथा युधि संग्रामे स स्तोतृणा-
> मनुग्रहाय प्रादुर्भवतीति शेषः । यद्यदा चाक्ष्मः ॥ चष्टेः क्षमतीर्वा ॥ सर्वस्य
> द्रष्टा सर्वसहो ना ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्वाजमन्नं मती मननीयं प्रशस्तं धना धनं च
> स्तोतृणामर्थाय भरते । बिभर्ति । वृष्टिद्वारा पोषयति । आदित् अनंतरमेव
> तप्यतुस्तापकः सूर्यो वृथा । अनायासवचनमेतत् । अनायासेन तपति । दीप्यते।
> ब्रह्मणस्पतिना दत्तैरन्नॆर्धनैश्च यज्ञस्य सिद्धत्वात् स्वयं केवलं हवींषि भुंजान
> एव वर्तत इत्यर्थः ॥

> *purohito devaiḥ purato 'tra sthāpitaḥ sa brahmaṇaspatiḥ saṃnayaḥ viśliṣṭānāṃ*
> *maṃtraiḥ saṃgamayitā bhavati | vinayaḥ saṃgatānāṃ vividhaṃ netā pṛthakkartāpi sa eva |*
> *tathā suṣṭutaḥ śobhanaṃ stotṛbhiḥ stutaśca bhavati | tathā yudhi saṃgrāme sa stotṛṇā-*
> *manugrahāya prādurbhavatīti śeṣaḥ | yadyadā cākṣmaḥ ‖ caṣṭeḥ kṣamatīrvā ‖ sarvasya*
> *draṣṭā sarvasaho nā brahmaṇaspatirvājamannaṃ matī mananīyaṃ praśastaṃ dhanā dhanaṃ ca*
> *stotṛṇāmarthāya bharate | bibharti | vṛṣṭidvārā poṣayati | ādit anaṃtarameva*
> *tapyatustāpakaḥ sūryo vṛthā | anāyāsavacanametat | anāyāsena tapati | dīpyate|*
> *brahmaṇaspatinā dattairannèrdhanaiśca yajñasya siddhatvāt svayaṃ kevalaṃ havīṃṣi bhuṃjāna*
> *eva vartata ityarthaḥ ‖*

Gist: Brahmaṇaspati, set in front by the gods as priest, joins and separates things by mantras, is well praised and comes to aid his praisers in battle; he brings food and wealth, and the sun shines effortlessly.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

पुरोहितः (पुरोहितः (*purohitaḥ*))—established at the head (of the gods); सः ब्रह्मणस्पतिः (सः ब्रह्मणस्पतिः (*saḥ brahmaṇaspatiḥ*))—that Brahmaṇaspati; संनयः (संनयः (*saṃnayaḥ*))—he who brings together, by the power of his mantras, things that are separate; विनयः (विनयः (*vinayaḥ*))—he who separates what is joined together; सः (सः (*saḥ*))—he; सुष्टुतः (स सुष्टुतः (*sa suṣṭutaḥ*))—is excellently praised; युधि (युधि (*yudhi*))—in battle; सः (सः (*saḥ*))—he is the helper of the praisers; यत् (यत् (*yat*))—when; चाक्ष्मी (चाक्ष्मी (*cākṣmī*)) (as supplied; the Saṃhitā has चाक्ष्मो (*cākṣmo*) and the Pada चाक्ष्मः (*cākṣmaḥ*))—the all-knowing Brahmaṇaspati; वाजं (वाजं (*vājaṃ*))—food; मती (मती (*matī*))—excellent; धना (धना (*dhanā*))—wealth; भरते (भरते (*bharate*))—he bears (for the praisers); आदित् (आदित् (*ādit*))—immediately thereupon; तप्यतुः (तप्यतुः (*tapyatuḥ*))—the burning; सूर्यः (सूर्यः (*sūryaḥ*))—the sun; वृथा (वृथा (*vṛthā*))—effortlessly; तपति (तपति (*tapati*))—shines.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

That same Brahmaṇaspati, established as priest of the gods, is the one who by the power of his mantras brings together things that are separate and separates what is joined. He is also excellently praised. In battle he is the helper. When Brahmaṇaspati bears food and excellent wealth for his praisers, immediately thereupon the burning sun shines effortlessly.

**Printed English**

He Brahmanaspathi, is the aggregator and subjugator (of objects); the family priest (of the gods); and the renowed [sic] in battle; the beholder (of all), who bestows food and desired treasures, whence the radiant sun shines without exertion.

### Printed p. 34

*Running head: [34 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24] (as supplied).*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

पुरोहितः ब्रह्मणस्पतिः संनयः (*purohitaḥ brahmaṇaspatiḥ saṃnayaḥ*)—"he brings together, by mantras, those who have been separated." Yāska explains the word *purohita* as "they place him in front" (Ni. 2-12). Sāyaṇa says "set in front by the gods": the gods keep Brahmaṇaspati before them. He describes this Brahmaṇaspati as one who, by the power of his mantras, brings together in one place those who are separated from one another. Further, विनयः (*vinayaḥ*) (the text has संगशानां (*saṃgaśānāṃ*) [?], evidently for the bhāṣya's संगतानां (*saṃgatānāṃ*)): "he too is the one who separates those who are joined" — he explains that, by reciting mantras, he can make those who are together, through affection or other causes, live apart.

सुष्टुतः युधि सः (*suṣṭutaḥ yudhi saḥ*) (the text has स्तोत्यणामनुग्रहाय प्रादुर्भवति (*stotyaṇāmanugrahāya prādurbhavati*)): being praised, he appears in person, to show his favour, when battle comes upon them.

For the word चाक्षृः (*cākṣṛḥ*) (so in the supplied text; the Saṃhitā and Pada have चाक्ष्मो (*cākṣmo*) / चाक्ष्मः (*cākṣmaḥ*)) the explanation is सर्वस्य द्रष्टा सर्वसहो ना (*sarvasya draṣṭā sarvasaho nā*): Brahmaṇaspati sees everything directly and endures all kinds of sorrows and calamities. Then वाजं मती धना भरते (*vājaṃ matī dhanā bharate*) is explained: for those who praise him he makes food and excellent wealth increase, bringing rain-waters, and so he nourishes them.

From this: since the sacrifice is accomplished by the foods and wealth given by Brahmaṇaspati, the burning sun is left merely enjoying the oblations himself (केवलं हवींषि भुंजान एव वर्तते (*kevalaṃ havīṃṣi bhuṃjāna eva vartate*)). That is, because Brahmaṇaspati carries out the sacrifices of the sacrificers with foods and wealth, the sun, who illumines and protects everything, merely receives his shares of the oblations and stays idle; this states the extent of Brahmaṇaspati's nourishing of those who depend on him.

#### Ṛk 10

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> वि॒भु प्र॒भु प्र॑थ॒मं मे॒हना॑वतो॒ बृह॒स्पतेः॑ सुवि॒दत्रा॑णि॒ राध्या॑ ।
> इ॒मा सा॒तानि॑ वे॒न्यस्य॑ वा॒जिनो॒ येन॒ जना॑ उ॒भये॑ भुंज॒ते विशः॑ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> विभु। प्रभु। प्रथमं । मेहनावतः । बृहस्पतेः । सुविदत्राणि । राध्या ।
> इमा । सातानि । वेन्यस्य । वाजिनः । येन । जनाः । उभये । भुंजते । विशः ॥ १० ॥

> *vibhu| prabhu| prathamaṃ | mehanāvataḥ | bṛhaspateḥ | suvidatrāṇi | rādhyā |*
> *imā | sātāni | venyasya | vājinaḥ | yena | janāḥ | ubhaye | bhuṃjate | viśaḥ ‖ 10 ‖*

### Printed p. 35

*Running head: [A. 2, A. 7, V. 2.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 35 (as supplied).*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> विभु विभूनि व्याप्तानि प्रभु प्रभूणि प्रौढानि प्रथमं प्रथमानि मुख्यानि ।
> एवंभूतानि मेहनावतो वर्षणवतो वृष्टिप्रदस्य बृहस्पतेर्बृहतो मंत्रस्य
> पालयितुः संबंधीनि सुविदत्राणि ॥ विदल्ग लाभे । सुविदेः कत्रन् । उ. ३ १०८. ॥
> सुष्टु लब्बव्यानि ॥ सुविदत्रं धनं भवतीति यास्कः ॥ राध्या राध्यानि सम्यक्
> साधनीयानि । प्राप्तव्यानीति यावत् । इमा सातानीमानि धनानि वेन्यस्य कमनीयस्य
> वाजिनोऽन्नवतो ब्रह्मणस्पतेः ॥ तृतीयार्थे षष्ठी ॥ ब्रह्मणस्पतिना दत्तानि । येन
> धनेनोभय उभयविधा जनाः स्तोतारो यजमानाश्च देवा मनुष्याश्च वा
> विशो निविष्टाः संतो भुंजते । भोगान् लभंते । तत्सर्वं ब्रह्मणस्पतिना
> दत्तमित्यर्थः ॥

> *vibhu vibhūni vyāptāni prabhu prabhūṇi prauḍhāni prathamaṃ prathamāni mukhyāni |*
> *evaṃbhūtāni mehanāvato varṣaṇavato vṛṣṭipradasya bṛhaspaterbṛhato maṃtrasya*
> *pālayituḥ saṃbaṃdhīni suvidatrāṇi ‖ vidalga lābhe | suvideḥ katran | u. 3 108. ‖*
> *suṣṭu labbavyāni ‖ suvidatraṃ dhanaṃ bhavatīti yāskaḥ ‖ rādhyā rādhyāni samyak*
> *sādhanīyāni | prāptavyānīti yāvat | imā sātānīmāni dhanāni venyasya kamanīyasya*
> *vājino 'nnavato brahmaṇaspateḥ ‖ tṛtīyārthe ṣaṣṭhī ‖ brahmaṇaspatinā dattāni | yena*
> *dhanenobhaya ubhayavidhā janāḥ stotāro yajamānāśca devā manuṣyāśca vā*
> *viśo niviṣṭāḥ saṃto bhuṃjate | bhogān labhaṃte | tatsarvaṃ brahmaṇaspatinā*
> *dattamityarthaḥ ‖*

Gist: These bountiful riches of the rain-giving Bṛhaspati are pervasive, mighty and chief; all who partake of them, praisers and sacrificers (or gods and men), enjoy what he has given.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

मेहनावतः (*mehanāvataḥ*)—of the rain-giver; बृहस्पतेः (*bṛhaspateḥ*)—of Bṛhaspati; सुविदत्राणि (*suvidatrāṇi*)—the riches; विभु (*vibhu*)—pervading everywhere; प्रभु (*prabhu*)—mighty; प्रथमं (*prathamaṃ*)—very excellent, they are; येन (*yena*)—by which wealth; उभये (*ubhaye*)—both kinds, men and gods; जनाः (*janāḥ*)—people; विशः (*viśaḥ*)—gathering together; भुंजते (*bhuṃjate*)—enjoy pleasures, such (riches too); राध्या (*rādhyā*)—worthy of worship; इमा सातानि (*imā sātāni*)—these riches; वेन्यस्य (*venyasya*)—of the beloved one; वाजिनः (*vājinaḥ*)—and possessor of food, namely Bṛhaspati — are given by him.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The riches of the rain-giving Bṛhaspati are pervasive everywhere, mighty and very excellent. Such riches, by which men and gods together enjoy pleasures, and which are worthy of worship, are given by Bṛhaspati, the beloved one, the possessor of food.

**Printed English**

Expansive, vast, and excellent riches, are the attainable (bounties) of Brihaspathi, the sender of the rain : these {are the gifts of the adorable giver of food, whereby both (descriptions of worshippers) entering here enjoy (abundance).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

मेहनावतः बृहस्पतेः सुविदत्राणि (*mehanāvataḥ bṛhaspateḥ suvidatrāṇi*) - वृष्टिप्रदस्य बृहतो मंत्रस्य पालयितुः सुष्टुलब्धव्यानि (*vṛṣṭipradasya bṛhato maṃtrasya pālayituḥ suṣṭulabdhavyāni*) (the bhāṣya, as supplied, has सुष्टु लब्बव्यानि (*suṣṭu labbavyāni*)). He is the one who pours rain-waters and protects all the abundant mantras

### Printed p. 36

*Running head: [36 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24] (as supplied).*

(Special Topics continued.) — thus explaining the greatness of Bṛhaspati or Brahmaṇaspati. Sāyaṇa explains the word सुविदत्र (*suvidatra*) as "that which is to be obtained in abundance" and gives its meaning as wealth.

Yāska gives the meaning सुविदत्रः कल्याणविद्यः (*suvidatraḥ kalyāṇavidyaḥ*) (Ni. 6-14), and explains सुविदत्रं धनं भवति (*suvidatraṃ dhanaṃ bhavati*) (Ni. 7-9), and quotes usages for both senses.

The wealth to be obtained from this Bṛhaspati — विभु, प्रभु, प्रथमं राध्या (*vibhu, prabhu, prathamaṃ rādhyā*) — is pervasive, mighty (abundant), chief, and something that must certainly be attained: thus the greatness of wealth is stated. And वाजिनः येन उभये जनाः भुंजते (*vājinaḥ yena ubhaye janāḥ bhuṃjate*): it is described that both kinds of people — the praising priests and the sacrificers, or men and gods — obtain the wealth bestowed by Bṛhaspati, the possessor of food, and live happily enjoying pleasures.

#### Ṛk 11

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> योऽव॑रे वृ॒जने॑ वि॒श्वथा॑ वि॒भुर्म॒हामु॑ र॒ण्वः शव॑सा व॒वक्षि॑थ ।
> स दे॒वो दे॒वान्प्रति॑ पप्रथे पृ॒थु विश्वेदु॒ ता प॑रि॒भूर्ब्रह्म॑ण॒-॑
> स्पतिः ॥ ११ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> यः । अवरे । पृजने । विश्वथा । विभुः । महां । ऊं इति । रण्वः । शवसा । ववक्षिथ ।
> सः । देवः । देवान् । प्रति । पप्रथे । पृथु । विश्वा । इत् । ऊं इति । ता । परिभूः ।
> ब्रह्मणः । पतिः ॥ ११ ॥

> *yaḥ | avare | pṛjane | viśvathā | vibhuḥ | mahāṃ | ūṃ iti | raṇvaḥ | śavasā | vavakṣitha |*
> *saḥ | devaḥ | devān | prati | paprathe | pṛthu | viśvā | it | ūṃ iti | tā | paribhūḥ |*
> *brahmaṇaḥ | patiḥ ‖ 11 ‖*

### Printed p. 37

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 3] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 37 (as supplied).*

(Ṛk 11 continues.)

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> विश्वथा सर्वप्रकारेण विभुर्व्याप्तो रणो रमयिता स्तोतव्यो वा यो
> ब्रह्मणस्पतिरवरे निकृष्टे वृजने वर्जनहेतुभूते बले वर्तमानं । दुर्बलमिति
> यावत् । महामु महांतं च ॥ छांदसो वर्णलोपः ॥ उभयविधं स्तोतारं
> शवसात्मीयेन बलेन ववक्षिथ । वोढुमिच्छति । सर्वदा वहतीति यावत् ॥ वहते?
> सनंतस्य लिट्येतद्रूपं । पुरुषव्यत्ययः ॥ स देवो दानादिगुणयुक्तो देवान्
> प्रति सर्वेषां देवानां । प्रतिनिधिः सन् पृथु पप्रथे । अत्यंतं प्रथते । यशसा
> विस्तीर्णो भवतीति यावत् । यस्मादेवं तस्माद्विश्वेदु सर्वाण्येव ता तानि सर्वाणि
> भूतजातानि परिभूः परिभवन् अभिभवन् वर्तत इति शेषः ॥

> *viśvathā sarvaprakāreṇa vibhurvyāpto raṇo ramayitā stotavyo vā yo*
> *brahmaṇaspatiravare nikṛṣṭe vṛjane varjanahetubhūte bale vartamānaṃ | durbalamiti*
> *yāvat | mahāmu mahāṃtaṃ ca ‖ chāṃdaso varṇalopaḥ ‖ ubhayavidhaṃ stotāraṃ*
> *śavasātmīyena balena vavakṣitha | voḍhumicchati | sarvadā vahatīti yāvat ‖ vahate?*
> *sanaṃtasya liṭyetadrūpaṃ | puruṣavyatyayaḥ ‖ sa devo dānādiguṇayukto devān*
> *prati sarveṣāṃ devānāṃ | pratinidhiḥ san pṛthu paprathe | atyaṃtaṃ prathate | yaśasā*
> *vistīrṇo bhavatīti yāvat | yasmādevaṃ tasmādviśvedu sarvāṇyeva tā tāni sarvāṇi*
> *bhūtajātāni paribhūḥ paribhavan abhibhavan vartata iti śeṣaḥ ‖*

Gist: Brahmaṇaspati, pervasive and delightful, wishes to bear by his own strength both the lowly and the eminent worshipper; being the foremost representative of the gods he is widely renowned and surpasses all beings.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

विश्वथा (*viśvathā*)—in every way; विभुः (*vibhuḥ*)—all-pervading; रण्वः (*raṇvaḥ*)—delightful; यः ब्रह्मणस्पतिः (*yaḥ brahmaṇaspatiḥ*)—which Brahmaṇaspati; अवरे (*avare*)—low; वृजने (*vṛjane*)—the worshipper who is in a miserable state; महामु (*mahāmu*)—the worshipper who is in an excellent state; शवसा (*śavasā*)—with his strength; ववक्षिथ (*vavakṣitha*)—wishes to bear; सः देवः (*saḥ devaḥ*)—he, the divine one; देवान् प्रति (*devān prati*)—in the eyes of the gods; पृथु (*pṛthu*)—exceedingly; पप्रथे (*paprathe*)—has become widely spread (by his fame he is renowned beyond all of them); विश्वेदु (*viśvedu*)—all; ता (*tā*)—beings; परिभूः (*paribhūḥ*)—surpassing, he is superior to all.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Brahmaṇaspati, who is all-pervading and delightful, wishes to bear with his strength both the worshipper in a low, wretched state and the worshipper in an excellent state. The same Brahmaṇaspati, being of divine nature, is more famed than all the gods. Surpassing all beings, he is superior to everything.

**Printed English**

He, who is all-pervading and delighting, is willing to sustain by his strength the noble worshipper, as well as one who is in low distress: greatly is that Brahmanaspathi renowned amongst the gods, and therefore is he verily supreme above all (beings).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

Brahmaṇaspati is pervasive in many ways; this god, who is a giver of happiness or worthy of praise, is, according to Sāyaṇa, वृजने—वर्जनहेतुभूते बले वर्तमानं दुर्बलं (*vṛjane—varjanahetubhūte bale vartamānaṃ durbalaṃ*)

### Printed p. 38

*Running head: [38 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24] (as supplied).*

(Special Topics continued.) — one who is weak, abandoned by all, and महांतं च (*mahāṃtaṃ ca*), one who is filled with greatness: it is described that he wishes to protect, by his own power, the worshippers of these two kinds; and that this god, who is endowed with qualities such as generosity, shines alone as the representative of all the gods (देवान् पप्रथे (*devān paprathe*)), and therefore his fame is widespread. विश्वेदु ता परिभूः - सर्वाणि भूतजातानि अभिभवन् वर्तते (*viśvedu tā paribhūḥ - sarvāṇi bhūtajātāni abhibhavan vartate*): it is concluded that this god shines, overcoming all creatures by his fame and by his power.

#### Ṛk 12

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> विश्वं॑ स॒त्यं म॑घवाना यु॒वोरिदाप॑श्च॒न प्रमि॑नंति व्र॒तं वां॑ ।
> अच्छें॑द्राब्रह्मणस्पती ह॒विर्नोऽन्नं॒ युजी॑व वा॒जिना॑ जिगातं ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> विश्वं । सत्यं । मघवाना । युवोः । इत् । आपः । चन । प्र । मिनंति । व्रतं । नां [?] !
> अच्छ । इंद्राब्रह्मणस्पती इति । हविः । नः । अन्नं । युजाइव । वाजिना । जिगातं ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

(No Ṛk number is printed after the Saṃhitā in the supplied text. The bhāṣya runs on to p. 39.)

> हे मघवाना । मघमिति धननाम । तद्वंताविंद्राब्रह्मणस्पती युवोरित्
> युवयोरेव विश्वं सर्वं स्तोत्रं सत्यं यथार्थं । स्तुत्या यद्यद्गुणजातं
> प्रतिपाद्यते तत्सर्वं युवयोर्विद्यमानमेव न त्वारोपितमित्यर्थः । तथा
> चनेत्येतत्पदद्वयसमुदायः । ऐकपद्यं त्वध्यापकसांप्रदायिकं यद्येव शब्दस्य
> पूर्वपदे न । अपश्च आपोऽपि । उपलक्षणमेतत् । एतदुपलक्षितानि सर्वाण्यपि
> भूतानि वां युवयोर्व्रतं कर्म न प्रमिनंति । न हिंसंति ॥ मीज् हिंसायां
> क्रैयादिकः । मीनार्तेनिगम इति ह्रस्वत्वं ॥ हे इंद्राब्रह्मणस्पती नोऽस्माकं
> हविरच्छाभिमुख्येन जिगातं । गच्छतं । आगच्छतमिति यावत् ॥ जिगातिर्गतिकर्मा ।
> जौहोत्यादिकः ॥ तत्र दृष्टांतः । युजीव वाजिना । युजा रथे नियुक्तौ वाजिनौ

> *he maghavānā | maghamiti dhananāma | tadvaṃtāviṃdrābrahmaṇaspatī yuvorit*
> *yuvayoreva viśvaṃ sarvaṃ stotraṃ satyaṃ yathārthaṃ | stutyā yadyadguṇajātaṃ*
> *pratipādyate tatsarvaṃ yuvayorvidyamānameva na tvāropitamityarthaḥ | tathā*
> *canetyetatpadadvayasamudāyaḥ | aikapadyaṃ tvadhyāpakasāṃpradāyikaṃ yadyeva śabdasya*
> *pūrvapade na | apaśca āpo 'pi | upalakṣaṇametat | etadupalakṣitāni sarvāṇyapi*
> *bhūtāni vāṃ yuvayorvrataṃ karma na praminaṃti | na hiṃsaṃti ‖ mīj hiṃsāyāṃ*
> *kraiyādikaḥ | mīnārtenigama iti hrasvatvaṃ ‖ he iṃdrābrahmaṇaspatī no 'smākaṃ*
> *haviracchābhimukhyena jigātaṃ | gacchataṃ | āgacchatamiti yāvat ‖ jigātirgatikarmā |*
> *jauhotyādikaḥ ‖ tatra dṛṣṭāṃtaḥ | yujīva vājinā | yujā rathe niyuktau vājinau*

### Printed p. 39

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 7, Varga 3] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 39 (as supplied).*

(Bhāṣya of Ṛk 12 continues, mid-word.)

> वेजनवंतावश्वावन्नं घासं प्रति यथा स्वयमेव शीघ्रं गच्छतस्तद्वत् शीघ्रमाग-
> च्छतमित्यर्थः ॥ युजेवेत्यत्र सुपां सुलुगिति प्रथमाद्वि वचनस्याकारः । तृतियायां
> हि सावेकाच इति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं ॥

> *vejanavaṃtāvaśvāvannaṃ ghāsaṃ prati yathā svayameva śīghraṃ gacchatastadvat śīghramāga-*
> *cchatamityarthaḥ ‖ yujevetyatra supāṃ sulugiti prathamādvi vacanasyākāraḥ | tṛtiyāyāṃ*
> *hi sāvekāca iti vibhakterudāttatvaṃ ‖*

Gist: "All praise is true of you both, Indra and Brahmaṇaspati; creatures (waters) do not violate your rite; come swiftly to our oblation like two harnessed horses to their fodder." The grammatical tail (the ending of the dual, accent of the instrumental) is not explained.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

मघवाना (*maghavānā*)—lords of wealth [as supplied, a stray "1" follows this word]; इंद्राब्रह्मणस्पती (*iṃdrābrahmaṇaspatī*)—O gods, Indra and Brahmaṇaspati; विश्वं (*viśvaṃ*)—all praise; सत्यं (*satyaṃ*)—in truth; युवोरित् (*yuvorit*)—is yours alone; आपश्च (*āpaśca*)—and all beings that rest on water; वां (*vāṃ*)—of you two; व्रतं (*vrataṃ*)—the rite; न प्र मिनंति (*na pra minaṃti*)—do not harm (do not transgress); युजा (*yujā*)—yoked to a chariot; वाजिना (*vājinā*)—horses; अन्नं इव (*annaṃ iva*)—as they go eagerly towards grass; नः (*naḥ*)—our; हविः (*haviḥ*)—to the oblation; अच्छ (*accha*)—facing; जिगातं (*jigātaṃ*)—come.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Indra and Brahmaṇaspati, lords of wealth, all praise is truly yours. All beings that rest on water do not [?] your rite. As horses harnessed to a पथ (*patha*)-[?] (the supplied text has पथक्कॆ (*pathakkè*) "to the path", probably for रथक्कॆ (*rathakkè*) "to the chariot") go eagerly towards grass, so come to our oblation.

(Doubt: the supplied text has मारुवुदिल्ल (*māruvudilla*) "will not sell" where the Pratipadārtha has "do not harm (transgress)"; probably मीरुवुदिल्ल (*mīruvudilla*) "do not transgress".)

**Printed English**

All truth, lords of wealth, (Indra and Brahmanaspathi), is inherent in you both : the waters injure not your rites: come to our presence (to receive) our oblations, like a pair of steeds (to receive) their provender.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this sūkta both Indra and Brahmaṇaspati are praised. मघवाना—तद्वंत् इंद्राब्रह्मणस्पती (*maghavānā—tadvaṃt iṃdrābrahmaṇaspatī*). The word मघ (*magha*) is stated by Yāska among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9). By this word Indra and Brahmaṇaspati are addressed as possessors of wealth.
युवयोरित् विश्वं सत्यं—युवयोरेव सर्वं स्तोत्रं यथार्थं । स्तुत्या यद्यद्गुणजातं प्रतिपाद्यते तत्सर्वं युवयोः विद्यमानमेव न त्वारोपितं (*yuvayorit viśvaṃ satyaṃ—yuvayoreva sarvaṃ stotraṃ yathārthaṃ | stutyā yadyadguṇajātaṃ pratipādyate tatsarvaṃ yuvayoḥ vidyamānameva na tvāropitaṃ*). Here the word विइल्लिश्व (*viilliśva*) [?] (as supplied, evidently विश्व (*viśva*) with a stray word run in) is given the meaning "all kinds (of every sort) of praises".

### Printed p. 40

*Running head: [Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24] (as supplied; the page number is garbled to "4)").*

(Special Topics of Ṛk 12 continued.) All the qualities stated in the Ṛks of Indra and Brahmaṇaspati are really present in them; they are not merely attributed, as poets say in their poems. In this way the abundant excellence of their qualities is described.

आपश्च वां व्रतं न प्रमिनंति—अपोऽपि । (*āpaśca vāṃ vrataṃ na praminaṃti—apo 'pi |*) Although the word आपः (*āpaḥ*) here names the waters, it is explained that all creatures pervaded by the five elements beginning with water (as supplied "ಉಪಕ", evidently for water) cannot destroy (cannot transgress) the obligatory duties performed by Indra and पृथ्विस्थितिगळु (*pṛthvisthitigaḷu*) (as supplied). The quoted Sanskrit has "ಏತದುಲಪಕ್ಷಿತಾನಿ" (as supplied).

नः हविः युजीव वाजिना अच्छ- (*naḥ haviḥ yujīva vājinā accha-*) ... "Just as swift horses yoked to a chariot go quickly towards their fodder, so may you come quickly." Just as the swift horses yoked to a chariot come with speed for their food, so may Indra and Brahmaṇaspati come quickly to receive the oblations and the like in our sacrifice; thus the prayer of the sacrificer is stated.

> उत अपि च आशिष्ठा आशुतराः शीघ्रगामिनो वह्नयः !! आश्वनामृतत् ॥
> वोढारो ब्रह्मणस्पतेरश्वा अनु शृण्वंति । अस्माभिः कृतं स्तोत्रमनुक्रमेण
> शृण्वंति । यद्वा ब्रह्मणस्पतिना कृतमनुशासनं शृण्वंति । आतस्ते तमस्मदीयं यज्ञं
> प्रापयंत्विति शेषः । सभेयः सभायां साधुः ॥ ढश्चंदसीति ढः ॥ विप्रो मेधावी

> सवर्णदीर्घः ॥ धना हविर्लक्षणानि धनानि तस्मै ब्रह्मणस्पतये भरते । बिभर्ति ।
> संपादयतीति यावत् । यद्वा स्तोत्रेण धनानि भरते । बिभर्ति पोषयति । आतो
> वीळुद्देषाः । वीळून् दृढान् प्रबलान् राक्षसादीन् द्वेष्टीति तादृशो ब्रह्मणस्पति-
> र्वशा वशाया गोः ॥ सुपां सुलुगिति षष्ठ्या लुक् ! ऋत्यक इति प्रकृतिभावः ॥
> ऋणमस्माभिर्यज्जभिः प्रदेयमवदानात्मकमनुक्रमेणाददिरादाता भवत्विति शेषः ।
> अवदानस्य ऋणत्वं च तैत्तिरीये त्रिभिर्यणवा जायते ब्रह्मचर्येण ऋषिभ्यः ।
> ६. ३. १०. ५. । इत्यादिना स्पष्टमाम्मातं । यद्वा अनुरानुगुण्ये । वशा वशस्य काम-
> स्याभिलाषस्यानुगुणमादाता भवत्विति योज्यं । हशब्दः प्रसिद्धौ । स खलु ब्रह्मणस्पतिः

> *uta api ca āśiṣṭhā āśutarāḥ śīghragāmino vahnayaḥ !! āśvanāmṛtat ‖*

> *adhvaryurhotā vā matī matyā mananīyena stotreṇa ‖ supāṃ sulugiti pūrva-*
> *savarṇadīrghaḥ ‖ dhanā havirlakṣaṇāni dhanāni tasmai brahmaṇaspataye bharate | bibharti |*
> *saṃpādayatīti yāvat | yadvā stotreṇa dhanāni bharate | bibharti poṣayati | āto*
> *vīḷuddeṣāḥ | vīḷūn dṛḍhān prabalān rākṣasādīn dveṣṭīti tādṛśo brahmaṇaspati-*
> *rvaśā vaśāyā goḥ ‖ supāṃ sulugiti ṣaṣṭhyā luk ! ṛtyaka iti prakṛtibhāvaḥ ‖*
> *ṛṇamasmābhiryajjabhiḥ pradeyamavadānātmakamanukrameṇādadirādātā bhavatviti śeṣaḥ |*
> *avadānasya ṛṇatvaṃ ca taittirīye tribhiryaṇavā jāyate brahmacaryeṇa ṛṣibhyaḥ |*
> *6. 3. 10. 5. | ityādinā spaṣṭamāmmātaṃ | yadvā anurānuguṇye | vaśā vaśasya kāma-*
> *syābhilāṣasyānuguṇamādātā bhavatviti yojyaṃ | haśabdaḥ prasiddhau | sa khalu brahmaṇaspatiḥ*
> *samithe | saṃyaṃti saṃgacchaṃte 'sminnāhutibhirdevā iti samitho yajñaḥ | tasminvājī*
> *annavān | tasmādbhava viṣa ādātā bhavatvityarthaḥ ‖*

Gist: The swift horses of Brahmaṇaspati hear our praise (or his command); the wise priest brings wealth with praise; may Brahmaṇaspati, destroyer of strong demons, accept our debt-like oblation, being rich in food at the sacrifice.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

उत (*uta*)—and; आशिष्ठाः (*āśiṣṭhāḥ*)—swift-going; वह्नयः (*vahnayaḥ*)—horses; अनु शृण्वंति (*anu śṛṇvaṃti*)—may they listen to our praises; सभेयः (*sabheyaḥ*)—excellent in the assembly; विप्रः (*vipraḥ*)—and wise, the Adhvaryu; मती (*matī*)—with a hymn worthy of thought; धना (*dhanā*)—the wealth in the form of oblations; भरते (*bharate*)—offers; वीळुद्देषाः (*vīḷuddeṣāḥ*)—Brahmaṇaspati, who hates the mighty demons; वशा (*vaśā*)—produced from the cow; ऋणं (*ṛṇaṃ*)—and offered by the sacrificers, the avadāna (offering); अनु आददिः (*anu ādadiḥ*)—may he accept (for the reason that); स ह ब्रह्मणस्पतिः (*sa ha brahmaṇaspatiḥ*)—that Brahmaṇaspati indeed; समिथे (*samithe*)—in this sacrifice; वाजी (*vājī*)—is possessed of food.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May the swift horses of Brahmaṇaspati listen to our praises. The Adhvaryu, excellent in the assembly and wise, offers with a hymn worthy of thought the wealth in the form of oblations. May Brahmaṇaspati, who hates the mighty demons, accept the avadāna (the oblation), which is produced from the cow and offered by the sacrificers. For that Brahmaṇaspati is indeed possessed of food at this sacrifice.

### Printed p. 42

*Running head: [42 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṃ. 2, A. 3, Sū. 24] (as supplied).*

**Printed English**

The very swift horses (of Brahmanaspathi) listen to (our invocation); the priest of the assembly offers with praise the (sacrificial) wealth: may Brahmanaspathi, the hater of the oppressor, accept (the payment of) the debt, agreeably to his pleasure : may he be the accepter of the (sacificial) [sic] food presented at this coremony [sic].

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

आशिष्ठा वयः अनु शृण्वंति— (*āśiṣṭhā vayaḥ anu śṛṇvaṃti—*) शीघ्रगामिनः ब्रह्मणस्पतेरश्वाः अस्माभिः कृतं (*śīghragāminaḥ brahmaṇaspateraśvāḥ asmābhiḥ kṛtaṃ*) स्तोत्रं अनुक्रमेण शृण्वंति । (*stotraṃ anukrameṇa śṛṇvaṃti |*) यद्वा ब्रह्मणस्पतिना कृतमनुशासनं शृण्वंति । (*yadvā brahmaṇaspatinā kṛtamanuśāsanaṃ śṛṇvaṃti |*) The horses of Brahmaṇaspati, moving with speed, listen to the praises of Brahmaṇaspati recited by the sacrificers or by the priests; or they listen to the command of Brahmaṇaspati. Therefore, the prayer of the sacrificers is stated: may those horses quickly bring Brahmaṇaspati to our sacrifice.

विप्रः मती धना भरते—मेधावी अध्वर्युः होता वा मननीयेन स्तोत्रेण (*vipraḥ matī dhanā bharate—medhāvī adhvaryuḥ hotā vā mananīyena stotreṇa*) हनिर्लक्षणानि धनानि पोषयति । (*hanirlakṣaṇāni dhanāni poṣayati |*) The word विप्र (*vipra*) is stated by Yāska (Ni. 3-19) among the names of the wise. Sāyaṇa gives two kinds of sense: that the wise Adhvaryu, or the priest called Hotṛ, recites excellent praises and gathers this abundant wealth for the sacrifice of Brahmaṇaspati; or that by praiseworthy hymns he wins the favour of Brahmaṇaspati (by his grace) and increases the wealth so acquired.

वीळुद्वेषाः वशा ऋणमाददिः— (*vīḷudveṣāḥ vaśā ṛṇamādadiḥ—*) Here the word वीळु (*vīḷu*) is read by Yāska among the names of strength (Ni. 3-9). By it the meaning is given that Brahmaṇaspati दृढान् राक्षसार्दी द्वेष्टि (*dṛḍhān rākṣasārdī dveṣṭi*) (as supplied), that is, subdues the demons and the like, who have abundant power. Thus Brahmaṇaspati, possessed of such greatness, अस्माभिः यज्ज्वभिः प्रदेयं अवदानात्मकं (हविः) (*asmābhiḥ yajjvabhiḥ pradeyaṃ avadānātmakaṃ (haviḥ)*) अनुक्रमेण आदाता भवतु (*anukrameṇa ādātā bhavatu*): the oblations that the officiating priests who perform the homa offer for him in the sacrifices (the materials given as a debt) are prayed to be accepted, as if a debt due were accepted without fail.

Here it may be said that the oblation-portion offered for Brahmaṇaspati is of the nature of a debt, and, since it must necessarily be offered, it is like a debt. Having explained the meaning thus, Sāyaṇa quotes: अवदानस्य ऋणत्वं च—जायमानो वै ब्राह्मणस्त्रिभिर्यणवा (*avadānasya ṛṇatvaṃ ca—jāyamāno vai brāhmaṇastribhiryaṇavā*) (the passage continues on the next page; the window ends here).

### Printed p. 43

जायते ब्रह्मचर्यण ऋषिभ्यो यज्ञेन देवेभ्यः प्रजया पितृभ्य एष वा अनृणो यः पुत्री यज्वा ब्रह्मचारिवासी (*jāyate brahmacaryaṇa ṛṣibhyo yajñena devebhyaḥ prajayā pitṛbhya eṣa vā anṛṇo yaḥ putrī yajvā brahmacārivāsī*) (Tai. Saṃ. 6-3-10-5). A twice-born man is born already bound by three kinds of debts: the debt to the sages, to the gods and to the fathers. The debt to the sages is discharged by studying the Veda in brahmacarya, the debt to the gods by performing sacrifice, and the debt to the fathers by begetting a son. Since this is said, Sāyaṇa explains that the oblation-portion (हविरवदान (*haviravadāna*)) is here spoken of by the word ऋण (*ṛṇa*) (debt), and he cites the Taittirīya Saṃhitā passage. And a second meaning is also given: यद्वा वशस्य कामस्य अभिलाषस्य अनुगुणवादाता भवतु (*yadvā vaśasya kāmasya abhilāṣasya anuguṇavādātā bhavatu*), that is, may Brahmaṇaspati accept the oblations offered by the priests according to the wish of each priest, in order to fulfil that priest's desire. In this sense the word वश (*vaśa*) is given the meaning "wish" (desire).

The word समिथे (*samithe*) is read by Yāska among the names of battle (Ni. 3-9). Sāyaṇa explains the word thus: संयंति संगच्छंते अस्मिन्नाहुतिभिः देवाः इति समिथो यज्ञः (*saṃyaṃti saṃgacchaṃte asminnāhutibhiḥ devāḥ iti samitho yajñaḥ*), that is, the rite in which the gods assemble, each with his own oblation-portion, is the sacrifice. तस्मात् हविष आदाता भवतु (*tasmāt haviṣa ādātā bhavatu*): therefore the prayer is explained to be that Brahmaṇaspati (together with all the gods) may be the receiver of the oblations in the sacrifices where all the gods gather together.

#### Ṛk 14

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ब्रह्म॑ण॒स्पते॑रभवद्यथाव॒शं स॒त्यो म॒न्युर्महि॒ कर्मा॑ करिष्य॒तः ।
> योगा उ॒दाज॒त्स दि॒वे वि चा॑भजन्म॒हीव॑ री॒तिः शव॑सासर॒त्पृथ॑क् ॥१४॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ब्रह्मणः । पतेः । अभवत् । यथावशं । सत्यः । मन्युः । महि । कर्म । करिष्यतः ।
> यः । गाः । उत्आजत् । सः । दिवे । वि । च । अभजत् । महीs इव । रीतिः । शवसा ।
> असरत् । पृथक् ॥ १४ ॥

> *brahmaṇaḥ | pateḥ | abhavat | yathāvaśaṃ | satyaḥ | manyuḥ | mahi | karma | kariṣyataḥ |*
> *yaḥ | gāḥ | utājat | saḥ | dive | vi | ca | abhajat | mahīs iva | rītiḥ | śavasā |*
> *asarat | pṛthak ‖ 14 ‖*

> निरगमयदित्यर्थः । स ब्रह्मणस्पतिर्दिवे द्युलोकाय तत्रत्येभ्यो देवेभ्य
> इत्यर्थः । व्यभजच्च । ता गा विभक्ताश्चाकरोत् । स च गोसमूहो महीव रीतिः ।
> महती स्तुतिरितिव ॥ रीज् स्रवणे ॥ यथा गंगादीनां स्रोतो विभक्तं सत् निम्म दे-
> शाभिमुखं प्रसरति तथा शवसात्मीयेन बलेन तत्तद्देवगृहं पृथक् विभिन्नोऽसरत् ।

> *mahi mahatkarma kariṣyato brahmaṇaspatermanyurmananasādhano maṃtraḥ krodho*

> *tītyarthaḥ | yo brahmaṇaspatiḥ paṇibhirapahṛtā gā udājat | udagamayat guhāyā*
> *niragamayadityarthaḥ | sa brahmaṇaspatirdive dyulokāya tatratyebhyo devebhya*
